> When they come to you ˹believers˺ they say, “We believe.” But they are committed to disbelief when they enter and when they leave. And Allah knows what they hide

**In With Disbelief, Out With Disbelief**

The verse describes men who come to the believers with the words "we believe", and then states what they carried through the door: they came in with disbelief and they went out with it. The saying and the state never met. The sūrah has already described people who say with their mouths what is not in their hearts (5:41 — *“O Messenger! Do not grieve for those who race to disbelieve—those who say, “We believe” with their tongues, but their hearts are in disbelief.”*), and the verse that follows describes men who race each other into sin (5:62 — *“You see many of them racing towards sin, transgression, and consumption of forbidden gain. Evil indeed are their actions”*). What this verse adds is the detail that being among the believers changed nothing: they sat in the gathering, heard the recitation, watched the community at prayer, and left with exactly what they had brought in.

**What the Words Were For**

The greeting was not an act of faith but an act of management. It kept the peace with a community that was growing and it protected their standing with their own people, and the verse treats that calculation as the whole of the matter. The Book gives the same picture of men who swear to the believers that they are with them when they are not (5:53 — *“˹Only then,˺ the believers will ask ˹one another˺, “Are these the ones who swore solemn oaths by Allah that they were with you?” Their deeds have been in vain, so they have become losers”*), and of a group that rises to prayer lazily, showing off to people and remembering God only a little (4:142 — *“When they stand up for prayer, they do it half-heartedly only to be seen by people—hardly remembering Allah at all”*). None of it is hidden from the One who is being addressed in the prayer they are performing.

**The Knowledge That Misses Nothing**

The verse closes with the sentence that God knows what they hide. The same statement appears in the sūrah of the Bee, where God knows what people conceal and what they make public (16:19 — *“And Allah knows what you conceal and what you reveal”*), and the Book uses the same word when it forbids the concealment of testimony (2:283 — *“And do not conceal the testimony, for whoever conceals it, their hearts are indeed sinful.”*). Hidden disbelief, hidden alliances and hidden mockery are all written down by the One who needs no confession to know what a heart is holding. The warning here is not that the men will be caught by a clever observer; it is that the front a person keeps up was never a hiding place to begin with.

---

## Verse 5:62

> You see many of them racing towards sin, transgression, and consumption of forbidden gain. Evil indeed are their actions

**Racing Towards Sin, Transgression, Forbidden Gain**

Wa tarā kathīran minhum yusāri'ūna fī al-ithm wa al-'udwān wa aklihim al-suḥt describes observable racing. The three — ithm, 'udwān, suḥt — cover inward sin, outward transgression, and unlawful wealth, showing comprehensive corruption. The same trio appears in *“Racing to sin, transgression, and forbidden gain”* (5:62) itself. The racing, yusāri'ūna, indicates eagerness, not reluctance, similar to racing to disbelief in verse 41 and racing for guardianship in verse 52, showing pattern of hastening to evil. The observation tarā, you see, indicates that this racing is visible, not hidden, so believers can recognize hypocrites by actions.

**Evil Indeed Are Their Actions**

La-bi'sa mā kānū ya'malūn — evil indeed are their actions — condemns. The same condemnation appears in *“Evil are their actions”* (5:62) and in *“Evil is their inaction”* (5:63) for rabbis who do not forbid. The phrase la-bi'sa is used for strong condemnation, as in *“Evil is what they did”* (5:79) for not forbidding evil. The condemnation shows that racing to sin, transgression, and forbidden gain is not minor but evil. The verse therefore judges outward actions that correspond to hidden disbelief described in previous verse: entering and leaving with disbelief leads to racing to sin.

**A Verse That Shows Outward Signs of Hidden Disease**

This verse shows outward signs of hidden disease: those with sickness in hearts racing for guardianship (5:52 — *“You see those with sickness in their hearts racing for their guardianship, saying ˹in justification˺, “We fear a turn of fortune will strike us.””*) now race to sin, transgression, forbidden gain. The outward racing is visible, tarā, you see, so believers can recognize hypocrisy by actions, not only by claims. The same visibility appears for those who swear oaths they are with you but deeds void, as verse 53 said. The verse's placement after verse 61's entering and leaving with disbelief shows that hidden disbelief manifests in racing to evil. The remedy is for rabbis and scholars to forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption, as next verse will command, indicating that leadership should prevent racing to sin.

**Why Racing Is Emphasized**

Yusāri'ūna, racing, stresses eagerness and competition in evil, as if they compete to do more sin, transgression, forbidden gain. The opposite is competing in good deeds, fa-istabiqū al-khayrāt, compete in good (5:48 — *“So compete with one another in doing good.”*), which is commanded for believers. The contrast shows two races: race to evil and race to good. Believers should race to good, not to sin. The verse therefore warns against racing to evil and encourages racing to good, as earlier verses commanded seek wasīlah and strive in Allah's way.

Suḥt, forbidden gain, is defined as bribery, as Ibn Mas'ūd said, and as payment for changing rulings, as al-Ḥasan said, and as usury, as some said, all forms of unlawful wealth that strips blessing. The same term appears in verses 42 and 62 for same group, showing that consuming forbidden gain is characteristic. The phrase la-bi'sa mā kānū ya'malūn, evil are actions, appears also in verse 62 itself and is among condemnations for those who race to evil.

---

## Verse 5:63

> Why do their rabbis and scholars not forbid them from saying what is sinful and consuming what is unlawful? Evil indeed is their inaction

**Evil Indeed Is Their Inaction**

La-bi'sa mā kānū yaṣna'ūn — evil indeed is their inaction — condemns inaction. The pair ya'malūn and yaṣna'ūn, both meaning to do, but yaṣna'ūn can imply crafting, making, indicating that leaders' inaction is crafted, deliberate, not accidental. The same condemnation appears in *“Evil is their inaction”* (5:63) and in *“Evil indeed was what they did”* (5:79) for not forbidding evil. The verse therefore teaches that religious leadership has duty to forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption, and failure is evil.

**Role of Rabbis and Scholars as Entrusted Keepers**

Al-rabbāniyyūn wa al-aḥbār, rabbis and scholars, were described in verse 44 as entrusted with Book and witnesses over it, with command not to fear people but fear Me and not trade revelations for small price. Their role is to preserve, judge by, and forbid violation of Book. When they do not forbid qawl al-ithm, sinful speech, and akl al-suḥt, consumption of forbidden gain, they betray trust. The same role appears for Muslims: *“Let there be among you community inviting to good, enjoining good and forbidding evil”* (3:104) commands enjoining and forbidding. The verse therefore applies to all religious leaders, Jewish, Christian, Muslim: they must forbid evil, not ignore for fear or gain. The question law lā, why not, indicates that forbidding is expected, and its absence is blameworthy. The next verse will describe Jews saying Allah is tight-fisted, another sinful speech that rabbis should forbid but did not.

**Sinful Speech and Unlawful Consumption as Two Evils**

Qawlihim al-ithm, saying sinful, refers to sinful speech such as lying, distorting scripture, saying Allah is tight-fisted (5:64 — *“˹Some among˺ the Jews said, “Allah is tight-fisted.””*), and claiming Allah is Messiah (5:17, 72 — *“Indeed, those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have fallen into disbelief.”*) or third of three (5:73 — *“Those who say, “Allah is one in a Trinity,” have certainly fallen into disbelief.”*). Aklihim al-suḥt, consuming forbidden gain, refers to bribery, usury, taking payment for distorting, as earlier defined. The two cover speech and wealth, showing that corruption is in both words and earnings. Leaders should forbid both, because sinful speech misleads people and unlawful consumption corrupts economy. The verse's pairing of qawl al-ithm and akl al-suḥt shows that they are linked: sinful speech often leads to unlawful gain, as distorting for bribery. The leaders' failure to forbid both allows racing to sin, transgression, forbidden gain described in previous verse to continue. The verse therefore links individual racing to evil and leadership failure to forbid, showing that both are needed for corruption to spread.

**A Verse That Condemns Leadership Failure**

This verse condemns leadership failure to forbid evil, which is among major duties. The same condemnation appears for Children of Israel who did not forbid one another from doing evil, as verse 79 will say *“They did not forbid one another from doing evil, evil was what they did”* (5:79). A verified ḥadīth in Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim (49) says whoever sees evil should change with hand, if not, with tongue, if not, with heart, and that is weakest faith, indicating that forbidding evil is duty. The verse's question law lā yanhāhum, why do they not forbid, is rebuke that should motivate leaders to forbid. The next verse will give example of sinful speech that should have been forbidden: saying Allah is tight-fisted.

The Qur'an says *“Those who were condemned among Children of Israel were condemned for disobedience and transgression, they did not forbid one another”* (5:78-79) showing that not forbidding leads to condemnation.

---

## Verse 5:64
> ˹Some among˺ the Jews said, “Allah is tight-fisted.” May their fists be tied and they be condemned for what they said. Rather, He is open-handed, giving freely as He pleases. That which has been revealed to you ˹O Prophet˺ from your Lord will only cause many of them to increase in wickedness and disbelief. We have stirred among them hostility and hatred until the Day of Judgment. Whenever they kindle the fire of war, Allah puts it out. And they strive to spread corruption in the land. And Allah does not like corruptors

**"God's Hand Is Tied"**

The verse records a sentence spoken by some of the Jews of Madinah: that God's hand is tied. The image is of a fist closed tight, and what the speakers meant was that God was holding back His provision from them. The reports connect the remark with their complaints in a time of hardship, and with their mockery of the Book's language about lending to God (2:245 — *“Who will lend to Allah a good loan which Allah will multiply many times over? It is Allah ˹alone˺ who decreases and increases ˹wealth˺. And to Him you will ˹all˺ be returned”*). The sūrah treats the words as an insult to God's generosity, and the Book records another version of the same insult elsewhere: the claim that God is poor while they are rich (3:181 — *“Indeed, Allah has heard those ˹among the Jews˺ who said, “Allah is poor; we are rich!””*).

**The Answer: Open Hands, Giving as He Pleases**

The verse answers with a correction and then a statement: rather, His hands are open, and He gives as He pleases. The plural in Arabic is the ordinary way of saying that a person is generous with both hands, and the verse tells the reader not to confuse withholding with stinginess: the same Book says that if God gave everyone unlimited provision, they would go beyond the bounds in the land, and that He sends it down in measure (42:27 — *“But He sends down whatever He wills in perfect measure.”*). A hand that gives at the right time and withholds at the right time is not a closed hand. And the Book treats the whole matter as an attribute of God that is affirmed as He intended it, without comparing Him to His creation: the same hand of God is over the hands of those who pledge to His messenger (48:10 — *“Surely those who pledge allegiance to you ˹O Prophet˺ are actually pledging allegiance to Allah. Allah’s Hand is over theirs. Whoever breaks their pledge, it will only be to their own loss. And whoever fulfils their pledge to Allah, He will grant them a great reward”*).

**The Revelation That Increases Some in Their Wrong**

The verse then says something that might surprise a reader: what has been revealed to the Prophet will only increase many of them in wickedness and disbelief. The revelation is not the cause; the reaction to it is. The Book has said the same of hearts carrying a disease, which the verses of God increase in disease (2:10 — *“There is sickness in their hearts, and Allah ˹only˺ lets their sickness increase. They will suffer a painful punishment for their lies”*), and the sūrah itself has described men who preferred this world and whose hearts were sealed (16:107 — *“This is because they prefer the life of this world over the Hereafter. Surely Allah never guides those who ˹choose to˺ disbelieve”*). What the verse teaches is that the same light which heals a willing reader hardens a reader who has decided to resist it.

**The Fire They Keep Lighting**

The verse closes with a picture of the community's political history: whenever they kindle a fire for war, God puts it out, and they work to spread corruption in the land, and God does not love those who spread corruption. The statement is not a prediction of one battle but a description of a pattern the Prophet's community would see played out: an alliance formed, an attack arranged, the plan collapsed. The verse's last sentence gives the reason for the failure, and it is not military: the One who dislikes corrupters does not lend His help to the work they do.

**The Community the Verse Was Speaking About**

The reports tie the passage to the tribe of Banū Qaynuqāʿ, the first of the Jewish communities of Madinah to break the agreement it had with the Prophet. The boast in the market, the breach of the treaty and the mocking of the Muslims' poverty belong to the same set of events, and the verses that follow this one describe the outcome: the men who trusted in their fortresses and their trade were expelled from the city, and the Book says of the community what it says of the pattern here — that whenever they lit the fire of war, God put it out. The verse's last sentence states the reason in the plainest terms. The Book does not attribute the failure to a weakness in the enemy's numbers or a mistake in their plans; it attributes it to a settled judgement about what God dislikes, and it adds immediately that God does not love the people who spread corruption. A few verses later the same passage holds out the opposite road: that if the People of the Book had believed and been mindful, the Book would have covered their bad deeds and brought them into gardens of bliss (5:65 — *“Had the People of the Book only been faithful and mindful ˹of Allah˺, We would have certainly absolved them of their sins and admitted them into the Gardens of Bliss”*). The two roads are set side by side so that the reader sees the door that stayed open even while the verse was being written.

---

## Verse 5:65

> Had the People of the Book only been faithful and mindful ˹of Allah˺, We would have certainly absolved them of their sins and admitted them into the Gardens of Bliss

**Absolving Sins and Gardens of Bliss as Reward**

La-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim, We would have covered evil deeds, indicates forgiveness that erases sins, and la-adkhalnāhum jannāt al-na'īm, admit into Gardens of Bliss, indicates reward. The pairing appears also for Muslims: forgiveness then great reward (5:9 — *“Allah has promised those who believe and do good ˹His˺ forgiveness and a great reward”*), and for People of Book here: absolution then Gardens of Bliss. The Gardens of Bliss, jannāt al-na'īm, appears also in *“Gardens of Bliss”* (5:65) and in *“In Gardens of Bliss”* (56:12) for righteous, indicating Paradise with bliss, not only gardens. The conditional law, if, shows that outcome depends on faith and mindfulness, not on lineage as People of Book claimed in verse 18 that they are children and beloved. The verse therefore corrects exclusivist claim: being People of Book does not guarantee Paradise; faith and mindfulness does, and had they had it, Paradise would have been theirs.

**A Verse That Offers Hope After Criticism**

This verse offers hope after strong criticism in verses 60-64 that listed worst punishment, cursed, transformed, etc. The hope is conditional: had they believed and been mindful, forgiveness and Gardens of Bliss. The same hope appears for all: *“Had they believed and been mindful”* (5:65) and *“Had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed”* (5:66) next verse, showing that following revelation leads to provision and forgiveness. The verse's placement after condemnation shows Qur'anic method: critique followed by invitation, as verses 15 and 19 did after describing breach, offering Messenger who clarifies. The invitation is general, not limited to past, so People of Book today can still believe and be mindful and attain Gardens of Bliss. The next verse will add that had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed from Lord, they would have been overwhelmed with provisions from above and below, showing worldly reward plus otherworldly.

**Why Faith and Mindfulness Are Paired**

Āmanū wa-ittaqaw, believed and were mindful, pairs faith and mindfulness, as many verses do, because faith without mindfulness may be claim without action, and mindfulness without faith may be ritual without foundation. The pairing appears for Muslims in *“Those who believe and are mindful”* (10:63) and for People of Book here, indicating same requirement for all. The same universalization appears in verse 69 which says believers, Jews, Sabians, Christians, whoever believes in Allah and Last Day and does good, no fear, nor grieve, showing that true success is by faith and good deeds, not by label.

**Gardens of Bliss as Abode of Delight**

Jannāt al-na'īm, Gardens of Bliss, na'īm from n-'-m, bliss, delight, indicates Paradise where bliss is continuous, not temporary. The phrase appears for righteous who believe and do good, as in *“Gardens of Bliss”* (5:65) and *“In Gardens of Bliss, upon thrones facing”* (37:44). The bliss includes provision, peace, and pleasure of Allah, which is greater, as *“Allah's pleasure is greater”* (9:72). The conditional law, if they had believed and been mindful, shows that bliss was possible for People of Book had they followed Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an, as next verse will say observing Torah, Gospel, and revelation leads to provisions from above and below. The verse therefore links faith and mindfulness to both forgiveness and bliss, offering alternative to curse and hostility.

The verse's promise la-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim, absolve sins, appears also for Muslims who believe and do good, as in *“We will absolve evil deeds”* (29:7).

The same pairing appears for Muslims in *“Believers, be mindful of Allah as He should be feared”* (3:102) and for all who attain success.

---

## Verse 5:66

> And had they observed the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to them from their Lord, they would have been overwhelmed with provisions from above and below. Some among them are upright, yet many do nothing but evil

**Observing Torah, Gospel, and Revelation Would Bring Provisions**

Wa law annahum aqāmū al-tawrāt wa al-injīl wa mā unzila ilayhim min rabbihim la-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim offers worldly reward for observing revelation. The same conditional appears in *“Had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed, they would have eaten from above and below”* (5:66) itself. The observing, aqāmū, means establishing, following, not merely reading, as *“Establish prayer”* (5:55) means perform properly. The reward is provisions from above and below, indicating worldly abundance that would have come had they followed guidance.

**Provisions From Above and Below as Abundance**

La-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim, they would have eaten from above and below, is phrase for overwhelming provisions from all directions: rain from above, crops and water from below. The same phrase appears for those who follow guidance, as in *“If they had followed right way, We would have given abundant water”* (72:16). The conditional law, had they observed, shows that they did not observe, so abundance did not come, and instead hostility and hatred were stirred, as verse 64 said, and many increased in wickedness when revelation came.

**Some Among Them Are Upright, Many Do Evil**

Minhum ummatun muqtaṣidah wa kathīrun minhum sā'a mā ya'malūn acknowledges minority upright. Minhum ummatun muqtaṣidah, among them is moderate community, muqtaṣidah from q-ṣ-d, to be moderate, balanced, just, indicates some are upright, moderate, not extreme. Wa kathīrun minhum sā'a mā ya'malūn, many do evil, sā'a from s-w-', to be evil, bad, mā ya'malūn, what they do, indicates many do evil, as previous verses described racing to sin, transgression, forbidden gain. The same acknowledgment appears in *“Among People of Book is upright community”* (3:113) and *“Some are upright, many do evil”* (5:66) itself, showing fairness: Qur'an does not generalize all as evil, but notes some upright, moderate, while many do evil. The upright are those who observed Torah and Gospel truly and recognized Prophet, as verses 82-85 will praise some Christians. The many who do evil are those who said Allah tight-fisted, stirred hostility, kindled fire of war, spread corruption.

**A Verse That Links Observance to Provision**

This verse links observance of revelation to provision, showing that following guidance brings worldly abundance, not only otherworldly reward. The link appears also in *“If people of towns believed and were mindful, We would have opened blessings from heavens and earth”* (7:96) similar. The observance aqāmū includes establishing, following, judging by, as earlier verses commanded judge by what Allah revealed. The reward la-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim, eat from above and below, indicates that provision would have come from all directions, not only one, showing generosity.

**Abundance as Result of Following Guidance**

The promise of eating from above and below shows that following guidance brings material abundance, not only spiritual, because divine law includes justice that leads to prosperity. The same promise appears for those who follow right way, they would have abundant water (72:16 — *“Had the deniers followed the Right Way, We would have certainly granted them abundant rain to drink”*), and for people of towns who believe, blessings from heavens and earth would open (7:96 — *“Had the people of those societies been faithful and mindful ˹of Allah˺, We would have overwhelmed them with blessings from heaven and earth.”*). The abundance from above includes rain, sun, and from below includes crops, minerals, water, indicating comprehensive provision. The verse therefore says that had People of Book observed Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an, they would have had worldly abundance plus Gardens of Bliss, showing that following revelation benefits both worlds.

---

## Verse 5:67

> O Messenger! Convey everything revealed to you from your Lord. If you do not, then you have not delivered His message. Allah will ˹certainly˺ protect you from the people. Indeed, Allah does not guide the people who disbelieve

**Convey Everything Revealed as Duty**

Yā ayyuhā al-rasūl balligh mā unzila ilayka min rabbik commands conveying. Yā ayyuhā al-rasūl, O Messenger, rasūl from r-s-l, to send, messenger, balligh, convey, from b-l-gh, to reach, convey to destination, mā unzila ilayka min rabbik, what was revealed to you from your Lord, indicates all revelation, not part, must be conveyed. The command is emphatic: convey everything, not some, as previous verses warned not to be lured away from some of what was revealed (5:49 — *“And beware, so they do not lure you away from some of what Allah has revealed to you.”*) and not to hide. The same command appears in *“O Messenger, convey what was revealed”* (5:67) itself.

**If You Do Not, You Have Not Delivered Message**

Wa in lam taf'al fa-mā ballaghta risālatah — if you do not, you have not delivered His message — warns that failing to convey part is like failing to convey all, because message is interconnected. Lam taf'al, you do not do, fa-mā ballaghta, then you have not conveyed, risālatah, His message, indicates that duty is to convey all, not selective. The same warning appears in *“If you do not, you have not delivered message”* (5:67) itself, showing that partial conveyance is considered non-delivery. The warning is severe to emphasize importance of conveying everything, even if it causes increase in wickedness for some, as verse 64 said revelation increases many in wickedness. The Messenger's duty is to convey, not to ensure acceptance, as *“Your duty is only to deliver clearly”* (5:99) will say. The verse therefore establishes that Messenger must convey even difficult rulings about food, ḥudūd, and alliances, despite opposition.

**Allah Will Protect You From People**

Wa Allāhu ya'ṣimuka min al-nās — Allah will protect you from people — promises protection. Ya'ṣimuka, protect you, from '-ṣ-m, to protect, preserve, min al-nās, from people, indicates that Allah will protect Messenger from harm of people who plot, as earlier verses described those who sought to harm and Allah held hands back (5:11 — *“O believers! Remember Allah’s favour upon you: when a people sought to harm you, but He held their hands back from you.”*) and those who kindle fire of war and Allah extinguishes (5:64 — *“Whenever they kindle the fire of war, Allah puts it out.”*). The same promise appears in *“Allah will protect you from people”* (5:67) itself. Early reports say Prophet was guarded by companions until this verse revealed, then he said leave me, Allah will protect me, as recorded in Sunan al-Tirmidhī (3046, graded ḥasan). The protection is both physical and from being prevented from conveying. The promise allows Messenger to convey without fear, because Allah protects from people who would harm for conveying.

**Allah Does Not Guide Disbelieving People**

Inna Allāha lā yahdī al-qawm al-kāfirīn — Allah does not guide disbelieving people — seals with lack of guidance for kāfirīn. Lā yahdī, does not guide, al-qawm al-kāfirīn, disbelieving people, indicates that those who persist in disbelief after revelation conveyed are not guided, because they chose disbelief. The same seal appears in many verses after warnings, as in *“Allah does not guide disbelieving people”* (5:67) and *“Allah does not guide wrongdoing people”* (5:51).

**Protection as Assurance for Conveyance**

Ya'ṣimuka min al-nās, protect you from people, is assurance that allows conveyance without fear. The same protection was shown when Allah held hands back from harming believers (5:11 — *“O believers! Remember Allah’s favour upon you: when a people sought to harm you, but He held their hands back from you.”*) and extinguished fire of war (5:64 — *“Whenever they kindle the fire of war, Allah puts it out.”*). Early reports say Prophet had guards from Anṣār until verse revealed, then he told them to leave, trusting Allah's protection, as Tirmidhī (3046) records. The assurance shows that Messenger's duty is to convey, and Allah's duty is to protect, so Messenger should not fear people.

**Convey, Even One Verse**

The duty this verse lays on the Prophet ﷺ is shared by his community in the measure each can carry: "Convey from me even one verse" (al-Bukhārī 3461). What the Messenger must not withhold of the Book, the community must not hoard of what it has understood of it. Read against this verse, the instruction shows that conveyance is not the work of elites alone: whoever knows one line and passes it on has fulfilled a portion of *balligh*, and silence about what one knows is the part of the duty that the verse's severe ending addresses.

---

## Verse 5:68
> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “O People of the Book! You have nothing to stand on unless you observe the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to you from your Lord.” And your Lord’s revelation to you ˹O Prophet˺ will only cause many of them to increase in wickedness and disbelief. So do not grieve for the people who disbelieve

**Nothing to Stand On Without the Book**

The verse gives the Prophet words to say to the People of the Book: you have nothing to stand on unless you establish the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to you from your Lord. The last of the three is the Qur'an, and the command to establish a book means to follow it, live by it and judge by what it says, not to keep it on a shelf. What the verse denies is any claim to a standing before God that is not carried by the revelation the claimant already has in his hands. A community that holds a Book and does not act on it has no foundation under its feet.

**The Three Books as One Chain**

The three are named together because the Book treats them as one line of revelation: the Torah given to Moses, the Gospel confirming what came before it, and the Qur'an confirming both and guarding them. The sūrah had already said what the earlier Books contained, in a verse that tells the People of the Book that if they had established the Torah, the Gospel and what was sent down to them from their Lord they would have eaten from above them and from beneath their feet (5:66 — *“And had they observed the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to them from their Lord, they would have been overwhelmed with provisions from above and below.”*), and the reward of the same obedience is named a few verses earlier as forgiveness and gardens of bliss (5:65 — *“Had the People of the Book only been faithful and mindful ˹of Allah˺, We would have certainly absolved them of their sins and admitted them into the Gardens of Bliss”*). The point is the same in each place. The blessing was attached to the practice, not to the possession.

**The Increase, and the Comfort to the Prophet**

The verse then says again what it had said a few verses before: what is revealed to the Prophet will only increase many of them in transgression and denial (5:64 — *“That which has been revealed to you ˹O Prophet˺ from your Lord will only cause many of them to increase in wickedness and disbelief.”*). This is not a contradiction of the message; it is the effect of light on a person who has decided to keep his eyes shut, and the Book has used the same picture of hearts carrying a disease which the revelation only makes worse (2:10 — *“There is sickness in their hearts, and Allah ˹only˺ lets their sickness increase. They will suffer a painful punishment for their lies”*). The verse closes with a word of comfort that the Prophet had heard before in the same sūrah when his own people refused him: do not grieve over a people who deny (5:26 — *“Allah replied, “Then this land is forbidden to them for forty years, during which they will wander through the land. So do not grieve for the rebellious people.””*). He had been told in the verse before this one to deliver the message in full and that God would protect him (5:67 — *“If you do not, then you have not delivered His message.”*); the outcome of the delivery was never part of his load.

---

## Verse 5:69
> Indeed, the believers, Jews, Sabians and Christians—whoever ˹truly˺ believes in Allah and the Last Day and does good, there will be no fear for them, nor will they grieve

**The Four Groups Named**

The verse lists the communities around the Prophet's message: the believers, the Jews, the Sabians and the Christians. It is the same list the Book gives in the Sūrah of the Cow (2:62 — *“Indeed, the believers, Jews, Christians, and Sabians—whoever ˹truly˺ believes in Allah and the Last Day and does good will have their reward with their Lord.”*), and it is given here right after verses that describe the corruption of the People of the Book, so that a reader does not take the criticism of a community as a verdict against every person inside it.

**The One Test That Applies to All of Them**

After the labels, the verse gives the single test: whoever believes in God and the Last Day and does good work. The label does not carry the promise; the faith and the deed carry it. Belief in God means His oneness along with trust in His messengers and books — the same covenant this sūrah has already described, where the Children of Israel were asked to believe in the messengers and support them (5:12 — *“Allah made a covenant with the Children of Israel and appointed twelve leaders from among them and ˹then˺ said, “I am truly with you.”*). Belief in the Last Day means living with the account in front of you. And doing good means the things the sūrah has been naming: the prayer, the alms, justice, honesty in contracts, and kind treatment of the people around you.

**No Fear and No Grief**

The reward is given in two halves: no fear for them, and no grief. The commentators read the first as the fear of what is coming and the second as grief over what is past — the two directions in which a person's peace can be destroyed. The Book gives the same promise elsewhere to those who befriend God (10:62 — *“There will certainly be no fear for the close servants of Allah, nor will they grieve”*) and to those who spend what they love in His way (2:262 — *“Those who spend their wealth in the cause of Allah and do not follow their charity with reminders of their generosity or hurtful words—they will get their reward from their Lord, and there will be no fear for them, nor will they grieve”*). What the verse refuses is the idea that a person is saved by the name he carries. It is the same sentence the Book gives to the Muslims in their own place: the label of Muslim is a name, and the deed is the thing it is a name for.

---

## Verse 5:70

> Indeed, We took a covenant from the Children of Israel and sent them messengers. Whenever a messenger came to them with what they did not desire, they denied some and killed others

**Covenant From Children of Israel and Messengers Sent**

Laqad akhadhnā mīthāq banī Isrā'īl wa arsalnā ilayhim rusulan reports covenant and messengers. Laqad akhadhnā, We took, mīthāq, covenant, firm bond, banī Isrā'īl, Children of Israel, as in verse 12, and arsalnā ilayhim rusulan, sent to them messengers, rusulan plural of rasūl, messenger, indicates many messengers sent to them, not one, as history shows many prophets among Israelites. The covenant included establishing prayer, paying alms, believing in messengers, supporting them, lending good loan, as verse 12 detailed, and not killing messengers. The sending of messengers is part of divine mercy: after covenant, messengers remind and clarify, as verse 15 said Messenger reveals much hidden. The same phrasing appears in *“We took covenant from Children of Israel and sent messengers”* (5:70) itself.

**A History of Denial and Killing**

The history of Children of Israel includes denying and killing prophets, as Qur'an says *“They killed prophets unjustly”* (2:61) and *“They killed prophets”* (3:21). Examples include Zakariyyā, Yaḥyā, and tries to kill 'Īsā, as *“They did not kill him nor crucify him”* (4:157) indicates try. The pattern kullamā jā'ahum rasūlun bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum shows that criterion for acceptance was desire, not truth: if messenger brought what souls desired, they accepted, if not, denied or killed. The same criterion appears for those who take rulings out of context, saying if you are given this, take it, if not, beware (5:41 — *“They distort the Scripture, taking rulings out of context, then say, “If this is the ruling you get ˹from Muḥammad˺, accept it.”*), showing desire-based acceptance. The verse therefore diagnoses that following desire leads to worst crimes: denying and killing messengers. The next verse will say they thought no consequences, turned blind and deaf, then Allah turned in forgiveness, but many became blind and deaf again, showing persistence.

Killing prophets is verified as among major sins of Israelites, as Qur'an says *“They killed prophets unjustly”* (2:61, 3:21 — *“Indeed, those who deny Allah’s signs, kill the prophets unjustly, and kill people who stand up for justice—give them good news of a painful punishment”*, 4:155 — *“˹They were condemned˺ for breaking their covenant, rejecting Allah’s signs, killing the prophets unjustly, and for saying, “Our hearts are unreceptive!”—it is Allah Who has sealed their hearts for their disbelief, so they do not believe except for a few”*) multiple times. The phrase bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum, with what souls do not desire, appears also in *“Whenever messenger came with what they did not desire”* (2:87, 5:70 — *“Whenever a messenger came to them with what they did not desire, they denied some and killed others”*) showing that desire-based acceptance is pattern. The verse's placement after universal principle shows that despite universal principle being clear, many fail due to following desire. The following verse will describe blindness and deafness as consequence of thinking no consequences. The covenant included prayer, alms, belief in messengers, support, good loan (5:12 — *“If you establish prayer, pay alms-tax, believe in My messengers, support them, and lend to Allah a good loan, I will certainly forgive your sins and admit you into Gardens under which rivers flow.”*), and messengers reminded of it. The response kullamā jā'ahum rasūlun bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum, whenever messenger came with undesired, they denied and killed, shows that guidance was rejected not for lack but for desire.

---

## Verse 5:71
> They thought there would be no consequences, so they turned a blind eye and a deaf ear. Yet Allah turned to them in forgiveness ˹after their repentance˺, but again many became blind and deaf. And Allah is All-Seeing of what they do

**They Thought Nothing Would Follow**

The verse names the thought that made everything else possible: they reckoned that no trial would come of it, that their denial and the killing of the messengers carried no consequence. The word for the trial covers testing, punishment and the division a punishment brings. Once the consequence was written out of the account, the rest followed at once — they turned blind and deaf, a picture the Book uses for men who cannot see the evidence or hear the warning. The Sūrah of the Cow describes another group in exactly those words: deaf, dumb and blind, and they will not return (2:18 — *“They are ˹wilfully˺ deaf, dumb, and blind, so they will never return ˹to the Right Path˺”*, and again at 2:171 — *“˹They are wilfully˺ deaf, dumb and blind so they have no understanding”*).

**Mercy That Followed, and the Relapse**

Then God turned to them in forgiveness. The reports of the earlier generations show what such mercy looked like: a people who had worshipped the calf, and when they turned back God accepted them (2:54 — *“Surely you have wronged yourselves by worshipping the calf, so turn in repentance to your Creator and execute ˹the calf-worshippers among˺ yourselves.”*). The verse then adds the sentence that makes it sobering — many of them became blind and deaf again. The word many is exact, not all: the sūrah has already said that among them there are men standing on the right way (5:66 — *“Some among them are upright, yet many do nothing but evil”*). The pattern of sin, repentance and relapse is the danger the passage describes, and the sūrah has spoken before of hearts that hardened (5:13 — *“But for breaking their covenant We condemned them and hardened their hearts.”*) and of a people who forgot the share of the reminder they had been given (5:13-14 — *“But for breaking their covenant We condemned them and hardened their hearts.”*). Forgiveness is not the same thing as a cure, and a man may be pardoned and still go back to the same wall.

**The Seeing That Misses Nothing**

The verse ends with the Name: God is All-Seeing of what they do. Everything the passage has named is inside that sight — the denial, the killing, the blindness, the turning, the relapse. The same Name closes other verses of the sūrah that speak of what people do (2:96 — *“You will surely find them clinging to life more eagerly than any other people, even more than polytheists. Each one of them wishes to live a thousand years. But even if they were to live that long, it would not save them from the punishment. And Allah is All-Seeing of what they do”*). The point of the ending is that the men who thought no trial would come were writing their record in front of a witness who never looked away, and the older dictionaries list the word for trial as testing, punishment and discord, which is how the verse uses it here.

---

## Verse 5:72

> Those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have certainly fallen into disbelief. The Messiah ˹himself˺ said, “O Children of Israel! Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord.” Whoever associates others with Allah ˹in worship˺ will surely be forbidden Paradise by Allah. Their home will be the Fire. And the wrongdoers will have no helpers

**Saying Allah Is Messiah as Disbelief Repeated**

Laqad kafara alladhīna qālū inna Allāha huwa al-Masīḥ ibn Maryam repeats judgment from verse 17: those who say Allah is Messiah have disbelieved. Laqad kafara, indeed disbelieved, as before, indicates that claim nullifies tawḥīd. The repetition from verse 17 shows emphasis, because claim is grave and appears among Christians, as earlier verses about Trinity will show. The Messiah himself, al-Masīḥ, said yā banī Isrā'īl u'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum — O Children of Israel, worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord — showing that Jesus himself called to worship Allah, not himself. The same call appears in *“Worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord”* (5:72) and in *“Indeed Allah is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him”* (3:51) from Jesus. The verse therefore refutes divinity claim by quoting Messiah's own monotheistic call. U'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum, worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord, is core message of all messengers, as *“We sent no messenger before you except We revealed to him, there is no god but Me, so worship Me”* (21:25).

**A Verse That Quotes Messiah to Refute Deification**

This verse quotes Messiah himself to refute deification, method of using opponent's accepted authority against claim. The same method appears for other claims: quoting Torah to refute distortion, as verse 44-45 did. The verse's repetition of laqad kafara from verse 17 stresses that claim is disbelief, not mere error, because it nullifies core of faith. A verified ḥadīth in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī (3445) says do not exaggerate about me as Christians exaggerated about son of Mary, linking exaggeration to deification, supporting verse's warning.

The principle man yushrik bi-Llāh fa-qad ḥarrama Allāhu 'alayhi al-jannah, whoever associates, Paradise forbidden, is among most severe warnings, and is used to show that shirk is unforgiven if not repented before death, as *“Allah does not forgive association”* (4:48). The phrase ma'wāhu al-nār, abode Fire, indicates that Fire is permanent home for those who die in shirk without repentance. The verse's quotation of Messiah's words yā banī Isrā'īl u'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum is also in Gospel of Mark 12:29 where Jesus says Hear O Israel, Lord our God is One, supporting monotheistic call. The following verse will discuss Trinity.

The same phrase appears for Jesus in *“Allah is my Lord and your Lord, worship Him”* (19:36) and for other messengers.

The phrase mā li-l-ẓālimīn min anṣār, no helpers for wrongdoers, appears also in *“Wrongdoers have no helpers”* (2:270, 3:192 — *“Our Lord! Indeed, those You commit to the Fire will be ˹completely˺ disgraced! And the wrongdoers will have no helpers”*) for similar.

---

## Verse 5:73

> Those who say, “Allah is one in a Trinity,” have certainly fallen into disbelief. There is only One God. If they do not stop saying this, those who disbelieve among them will be afflicted with a painful punishment

**Saying Allah Is Third of Three as Disbelief**

Laqad kafara alladhīna qālū inna Allāha thālithu thalāthah repeats judgment for Trinity claim. The same judgment appears in *“Those who say Allah is third of three have disbelieved”* (5:73) itself. The claim is similar to previous verse's claim Allah is Messiah, but adds third, indicating more complex association. The verse therefore refutes Trinity as disbelief, not mere error, because it nullifies tawḥīd.

**There Is Only One God as Correction**

Wa mā min ilāhin illā ilāhun wāḥid — there is no god except One God — corrects. The same correction appears in *“There is only One God”* (5:73) and in *“Your God is One God”* (2:163) and *“Say, He is Allah, One”* (112:1). The oneness is not only in number but at bottom, attributes, and right to worship. The Trinity claim says three, but verse says only One, so three is false. The correction is simple and direct: no god except One, so claim of third of three is disbelief. The next clause warns that if they do not stop, painful punishment will afflict disbelievers among them. Wa in lam yantahū 'ammā yaqūlūna la-yamassanna alladhīna kafarū minhum 'adhābun alīm — if they do not stop saying this, those who disbelieve among them will be afflicted with painful punishment — warns. The same warning appears in *“If they do not stop, painful punishment will afflict disbelievers”* (5:73) itself.

**Why Trinity Is Judged as Disbelief**

Trinity is judged as disbelief because it says three gods while Allah is One, and because it associates Messiah and Holy Spirit with Allah in divinity, which is shirk, association, as verse 72 said whoever associates, Paradise forbidden, Fire abode. The Qur'an says *“They disbelieve who say Allah is third of three, there is only One God”* (5:73) itself, linking disbelief to saying three. The teaching about Trinity developed after Jesus, with councils, and was not taught by Jesus himself, who said worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord (5:72 — *“Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord.””*). The verse therefore returns to Messiah's own monotheistic call to refute Trinity. The next verse will call to repentance and forgiveness, offering exit. The verse therefore offers chance to stop, as earlier verses offered forgiveness for repentance after wrongdoing and mending (5:39 — *“But whoever repents after their wrongdoing and mends their ways, Allah will surely turn to them in forgiveness. Indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful”*) and for repentance before seizure (5:34 — *“As for those who repent before you seize them, then know that Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful”*). The next verse will ask will they not turn to Allah in repentance and seek forgiveness, Allah is Forgiving, Merciful, offering exit from Trinity claim.

---

## Verse 5:74

> Will they not turn to Allah in repentance and seek His forgiveness? And Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful

**Will They Not Turn in Repentance and Seek Forgiveness**

A-fa-lā yatūbūna ilā Allāhi wa yastaghfirūnah asks if they will not repent and seek forgiveness after Trinity and Messiah claims. A-fa-lā, will not, interrogative with fa- and lā, yatūbūna ilā Allāh, turn to Allah in repentance, tābū from t-w-b, to return, repent, wa yastaghfirūnah, seek His forgiveness, yastaghfirūn from gh-f-r, to seek covering, forgiveness, indicates that after warning of painful punishment for those who do not stop saying Trinity (5:73 — *“There is only One God. If they do not stop saying this, those who disbelieve among them will be afflicted with a painful punishment”*) and after Paradise forbidden for associating (5:72 — *“Whoever associates others with Allah ˹in worship˺ will surely be forbidden Paradise by Allah.”*), they are invited to repent and seek forgiveness. The same question appears in *“Will they not turn to Allah in repentance and seek forgiveness”* (5:74) itself, offering hope. The question is rhetorical, encouraging repentance: will they not turn, implying they should. The invitation shows that even for grave claims like Allah is Messiah and third of three, repentance and forgiveness remain possible before death, as Allah is Forgiving, Merciful.

**Allah Is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful as Seal**

Wa Allāhu Ghafūr Raḥīm — Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful — seals with two names that offer hope after severe warnings. Ghafūr, intensely forgiving, and Raḥīm, Most Merciful, as before, indicate that Allah forgives those who repent and shows mercy. The same seal appears after many repentance invitations, as in *“Allah is Forgiving, Merciful”* (5:74) itself and *“Allah is Forgiving, Merciful”* (5:34, 5:39 — *“But whoever repents after their wrongdoing and mends their ways, Allah will surely turn to them in forgiveness. Indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful”*) for ḥirābah and theft. The seal shows that forgiveness is possible even for shirk if repented before death, as *“Allah does not forgive association. Forgives less than that for whom He wills”* (4:48) refers to those who die without repentance, but those who repent before death are forgiven, as *“Say to those who disbelieve, if they cease, what has passed will be forgiven”* (8:38) states. The verse therefore offers exit from Trinity and Messiah claims through repentance and seeking forgiveness.

**A Verse That Offers Hope After Warning**

This verse offers hope after warnings in verses 72-73 that Paradise forbidden for associating and painful punishment for Trinity if not stopped. The hope is conditional on turning in repentance and seeking forgiveness, yatūbūna wa yastaghfirūnah, turn and seek forgiveness. The same hope appears after other severe warnings, such as after describing disbelievers who cannot ransom even with everything doubled (5:36-37 — *“And they will suffer a painful punishment”*), after theft penalty (5:39 — *“But whoever repents after their wrongdoing and mends their ways, Allah will surely turn to them in forgiveness. Indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful”*) offers repentance and mending, and after ḥirābah (5:34 — *“As for those who repent before you seize them, then know that Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful”*) offers repentance before seizure. The pattern shows that Islamic law pairs warning with hope, severity with mercy, to encourage reform. The question a-fa-lā yatūbūna, will they not turn, is gentle, inviting, not condemning, after strong condemnation, showing that purpose of condemnation is to lead to repentance, not to despair.

**Why Repentance and Seeking Forgiveness Are Paired**

Yatūbūna ilā Allāh wa yastaghfirūnah, turn to Allah and seek forgiveness, pairs two acts: turning, tawbah, and seeking forgiveness, istighfār. Tawbah is returning from sin to obedience, and istighfār is seeking covering of sin so it does not lead to punishment. The pairing appears often, as in *“Turn to Allah and seek forgiveness”* (5:74) and *“Seek forgiveness and turn to Him”* (11:3). The order here — turn then seek forgiveness — indicates that turning is first, then seeking forgiveness, though often order reversed.

**Turning to Allah After Grave Claims**

Yatūbūna ilā Allāh, turn to Allah, indicates turning from Trinity and Messiah claims to monotheism, as Messiah himself said worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord (5:72 — *“Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord.””*). The turning is from associating to worshipping One God, as verse 73 corrected there is only One God. The same invitation appears for those who said Allah tight-fisted (5:64 — *“˹Some among˺ the Jews said, “Allah is tight-fisted.””*) and for those who waged war, showing that no claim is beyond forgiveness if repented.

---

## Verse 5:75
> The Messiah, son of Mary, was no more than a messenger. ˹Many˺ messengers had ˹come and˺ gone before him. His mother was a woman of truth. They both ate food. See how We make the signs clear to them, yet see how they are deluded ˹from the truth˺

**A Messenger and a Woman of Truth**

The verse answers the claims made about the Messiah and his mother by saying what the two of them were. The Messiah, son of Mary, was no more than a messenger, and messengers had come and gone before him. His mother is described as a woman of truth — an honour given to her honesty and devotion, not a share in divinity. Both descriptions keep the two inside the human family, and the sūrah has already shown the same Messiah telling his people to worship God, his Lord and theirs (5:72 — *“Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord.””*).

**The Evidence Anyone Can Check: They Both Ate Food**

Then the verse gives the plainest proof there is: the two of them ate food. Eating is need, and a man who eats is fed by someone else, so the One who provides for all cannot be a man who needs provision himself. The Book says of God that He feeds and is not fed (6:14 — *“Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “Will I take any guardian other than Allah, the Originator of the heavens and the earth, Who provides for all and is not in need of provision?””*), and the Sūrah of Purity describes Him as the One on whom all things depend, who begets not and is not begotten (112:2-3 — *“Allah—the Sustainer ˹needed by all˺ He has never had offspring, nor was He born”*). The argument needs no scholar to follow. It asks the reader to look at the two people in front of him and notice the ordinary facts of a human life — which is the Book's method against the idols too, when it challenges them to create a single fly even with all their forces gathered for it (22:73 — *“And if a fly were to snatch anything away from them, they cannot ˹even˺ retrieve it from the fly.”*).

**Signs Made Clear, and Eyes That Turn Away**

The verse closes with a double look: see how We make the signs clear to them, then see how they are turned away from them. The Book uses the same word for people who see evidence and slide past it (6:46 — *“See ˹O Prophet˺ how We vary the signs, yet they still turn away”*). The question the verse leaves with the reader is not whether the proof is available, but why a proof so clear fails to settle a matter — and the answer the sūrah gives throughout is that a man who has already decided what he wants to believe has closed the door himself.

---

## Verse 5:76

> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “How can you worship besides Allah those who can neither harm nor benefit you? And Allah ˹alone˺ is the All-Hearing, All-Knowing.”

**How Can You Worship Those Who Cannot Harm Nor Benefit**

Qul a-ta'budūna min dūni Allāhi mā lā yamliku lakum ḍarran wa lā naf'an questions worship of those besides Allah who cannot harm nor benefit. The same question appears in *“How can you worship besides Allah what cannot harm nor benefit”* (5:76) itself and in *“Do you worship besides Allah what cannot harm nor benefit”* (21:66) for idols. The argument is from power: worship should be for One who can harm and benefit, not for those who cannot. Messiah and mother ate food and needed, so cannot harm nor benefit, as previous verse said they both ate food, showing neediness.

**Allah Is All-Hearing, All-Knowing as True Deity**

Wa Allāhu huwa al-Samī' al-'Alīm — Allah is All-Hearing, All-Knowing — gives true deity's attributes. Samī', All-Hearing, from s-m-', to hear, and 'Alīm, All-Knowing, from '-l-m, to know, indicates Allah hears all supplications and knows all needs, so He can harm and benefit, unlike those who cannot. The same pair appears in many verses after refuting false worship, as in *“Allah is All-Hearing, All-Knowing”* (5:76) itself and *“Allah is All-Hearing, All-Knowing”* (2:224) etc. The attributes are appropriate here: worship requires hearing supplication and knowing need, which Messiah and mother, being human who ate food, cannot do for all, but Allah can. The verse therefore contrasts false deities who cannot harm nor benefit with true deity who is All-Hearing, All-Knowing, so worship should be for Him alone.

**A Verse That Questions False Worship**

This verse questions false worship with simple criterion: can they harm or benefit? If not, why worship. The criterion is accessible: worship should be for One who can harm and benefit, not for those who cannot. The verse's placement after verse 75's they both ate food shows that eating food implies inability to harm and benefit independently, because needy cannot benefit others. The next verse will warn People of Book not to go to extremes beyond truth nor follow vain desires of those who went astray before, misled many, strayed from right way, continuing refutation of exaggeration about Messiah.

**Why Harm and Benefit Are Mentioned Together**

Ḍarr and naf', harm and benefit, are mentioned together to cover all power: harming enemies and benefiting allies, both require ownership and control, yamliku, which false deities lack. The same pair appears for idols: *“They cannot harm nor benefit”* (10:18) and for those who cannot own harm nor benefit for themselves, as *“They do not own for themselves harm nor benefit”* (25:3) for idols. The pair shows that true deity must be able to do both, while false cannot do either. The verse therefore argues that worshipping those who cannot harm nor benefit is irrational, while worshipping Allah who is All-Hearing, All-Knowing, who can harm and benefit, is rational. The following verse will warn against going to extremes beyond truth, which is what deifying Messiah is: extreme beyond truth.

The same criterion appears for idols: *“They cannot harm nor benefit”* (25:55) and for Messiah and mother who ate food and needed, so cannot harm nor benefit.

---

## Verse 5:77

> Say, “O People of the Book! Do not go to extremes in your faith beyond the truth, nor follow the vain desires of those who went astray before ˹you˺. They misled many and strayed from the Right Way.”

**Do Not Go to Extremes Beyond Truth**

Qul yā ahl al-kitāb lā taghlū fī dīnikum ghayra al-ḥaqq warns against ghuluw, going to extremes beyond truth. Qul, say, yā ahl al-kitāb, O People of Book, lā taghlū, do not go to extremes, taghlū from gh-l-w, to exceed, go beyond limit, fī dīnikum, in your religion, faith, ghayra al-ḥaqq, beyond truth, other than truth, indicates exceeding limits in religion beyond truth, such as deifying Messiah, saying Allah is Messiah (5:17, 72 — *“Indeed, those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have fallen into disbelief.”*) or third of three (5:73 — *“Those who say, “Allah is one in a Trinity,” have certainly fallen into disbelief.”*), which is extreme beyond truth that Messiah was only messenger, mother woman of truth, both ate food (5:75 — *“His mother was a woman of truth.”*). The same warning appears in *“Do not go to extremes in your religion”* (4:171) for People of Book about Messiah, showing that ghuluw about Messiah is prohibited. The verse therefore warns against extremes that deify or unduly lower, and commands staying upon truth, ḥaqq.

**Do Not Follow Vain Desires of Those Who Went Astray Before**

Wa lā tattabi'ū ahwā' qawmin qad ḍallū min qabl warns against following desires of those who went astray before. The same warning appears in *“Do not follow desires of those who went astray before”* (5:77) itself and in *“Do not follow desires of those who went astray”* (5:77) similar. The desires, ahwā', are vain, not based on truth, and following them leads to straying, as previous verses about distorting and forgetting portion showed. The verse therefore warns against blind imitation of previous astray, which is how extremes spread.

**They Misled Many and Strayed From Right Way**

Wa aḍallū kathīran wa ḍallū 'an sawā' al-sabīl — they misled many and strayed from right way — describes consequence of following desires and extremes. Aḍallū kathīran, misled many, aḍallū from ḍ-l-l, to mislead, cause to stray, kathīran, many, indicates that those who went astray before misled many others, not only themselves. Ḍallū 'an sawā' al-sabīl, strayed from right way, sawā' al-sabīl as in verses 12 and 60, even, right way, indicates they themselves strayed from right way. The same phrase appears in *“Misled many and strayed from right way”* (5:77) itself. The two — misleading many and straying themselves — show that following desire and extremes harms both self and others. The verse therefore warns that extremes not only cause self to stray but cause many to stray, so they should be avoided. The next verse will say disbelievers among Children of Israel were condemned in revelations of David and Jesus for disobedience and violations, showing consequence of straying.

Ghuluw, going to extremes, from ghalā, to exceed, go beyond, is defined as exceeding limits in religion, such as deifying prophets or denying them. Lisān al-'Arab defines ghuluw as exceeding limit. The same term appears in ḥadīth warning against extremes, as in Nasā'ī (3057). The phrase ahwā' qawmin qad ḍallū min qabl, desires of people who went astray before, refers to previous generations who went astray by following desire, such as those who deified 'Uzayr and Messiah, as in *“Jews said 'Uzayr is son of Allah, Christians said Messiah is son”* (9:30). The following verse will condemn disbelievers among Children of Israel.

---

## Verse 5:78
> The disbelievers among the Children of Israel were condemned in the revelations of David and Jesus, son of Mary. That was for their disobedience and violations

**Disbelievers Among Children of Israel Condemned in David and Jesus Revelations**

Lu'ina alladhīna kafarū min banī Isrā'īl 'alā lisāni Dāwūd wa 'Īsā ibn Maryam reports curse. Lu'ina, condemned, cursed, from l-'-n, to expel from mercy, alladhīna kafarū min banī Isrā'īl, disbelievers among Children of Israel, 'alā lisāni Dāwūd wa 'Īsā ibn Maryam, in revelations or tongues of David and Jesus, son of Mary, lisān, tongue, language, revelation, indicates that curse was pronounced by David and Jesus, in Zabūr and Gospel, or through their tongues. The same phrasing appears in *“Disbelievers among Children of Israel were cursed by tongue of David and Jesus”* (5:78) itself. The curse was for persistent disobedience and violations, as next clause says. Early reports say David cursed those who violated Sabbath and were transformed into apes, as in *“Be apes despised”* (2:65, 7:166 — *“But when they stubbornly persisted in violation, We said to them, “Be disgraced apes!””*), and Jesus cursed those who disbelieved among Israelites, as in Gospel. The verse therefore shows that curse came from prophets of their own, David and Jesus, not only from Muḥammad, indicating that disbelief among Israelites was condemned by their own prophets.

**For Disobedience and Violations as Reason**

Dhālika bi-mā 'aṣaw wa kānū ya'tadūn — that was for disobedience and violations — gives reason: bi-mā 'aṣaw. Disobedience, 'aṣaw from '-ṣ-y, to disobey, and kānū ya'tadūn, they transgressed, ya'tadūn from '-d-w, to transgress, exceed limits, indicates that curse was for persistent disobedience to commands and transgression of limits, such as violating Sabbath, killing prophets, consuming forbidden gain, and not forbidding evil. The same reason appears in *“That was for disobedience and violations”* (5:78) and in *“That was for disobedience and transgression”* (2:61) for similar. The disobedience includes refusing to enter Holy Land (5:24 — *“˹Yet˺ they said, “O Moses! ˹Still˺ we will never enter as long as they remain there. So go—both you and your Lord—and fight; we are staying right here!””*), saying Allah tight-fisted (5:64 — *“˹Some among˺ the Jews said, “Allah is tight-fisted.””*), and saying Allah is Messiah or third of three (5:17, 72, 73 — *“Indeed, those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have fallen into disbelief.”*), all forms of disobedience. The violations include exceeding limits in law, such as having unequal penalties and taking rulings out of context. The curse therefore is for disobedience and transgression, not arbitrary.

**A Verse That Shows Curse From Own Prophets**

This verse shows that disbelievers among Children of Israel were cursed by David and Jesus, prophets from among them, indicating that curse is not only from outside but from inside, from their own prophets who knew them. The curse by David and Jesus is recorded in Zabūr and Gospel, though not in current texts in same wording, but Qur'an affirms it as part of revelation. The reason disobedience and violations shows that curse is for deeds, not for lineage. The same reason appears for other curses: for breaking covenant, We cursed and hardened hearts (5:13 — *“But for breaking their covenant We condemned them and hardened their hearts.”*). The verse therefore links previous criticisms — breaking covenant, distorting, forgetting portion, racing to sin, not forbidding evil, saying Allah tight-fisted — to curse by David and Jesus, showing that those deeds warrant curse from their own prophets. The next verse will say they did not forbid one another from doing evil, evil was what they did, showing specific violation that led to curse.

**Disobedience and Violations as General Reason**

The reason given here, that they disobeyed and went on transgressing, covers many particular violations named in the sūrah: breaking the covenant (5:13 records it — *“But for breaking their covenant We condemned them and hardened their hearts.”*), distorting words (5:13 describes it — *“But for breaking their covenant We condemned them and hardened their hearts.”*) and (5:41 describes it — *“O Messenger! Do not grieve for those who race to disbelieve—those who say, “We believe” with their tongues, but their hearts are in disbelief.”*), forgetting the portion they were given (5:13 states it — *“They distorted the words of the Scripture and neglected a portion of what they had been commanded to uphold.”*) and (5:14 states it — *“We took their covenant, but they neglected a portion of what they had been commanded to uphold.”*), racing one another towards rejection (5:41 describes it — *“O Messenger! Do not grieve for those who race to disbelieve—those who say, “We believe” with their tongues, but their hearts are in disbelief.”*), listening to lies and consuming what is forbidden (5:42 describes it — *“They eagerly listen to falsehood and consume forbidden gain.”*) and (5:62 describes it — *“You see many of them racing towards sin, transgression, and consumption of forbidden gain. Evil indeed are their actions”*), failing to forbid what is wrong (5:63 describes it — *“Why do their rabbis and scholars not forbid them from saying what is sinful and consuming what is unlawful? Evil indeed is their inaction”*) and (5:79 describes it — *“They did not forbid one another from doing evil. Evil indeed was what they did”*), saying that God's hand is tied (5:64 records the claim — *“˹Some among˺ the Jews said, “Allah is tight-fisted.””*), kindling the fire of war and spreading corruption (5:64 states it — *“And they strive to spread corruption in the land.”*), and saying that God is the Messiah, or the third of three (5:17 records it — *“Indeed, those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have fallen into disbelief.”*) and (5:72 records it — *“Those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have certainly fallen into disbelief.”*) and (5:73 records it — *“There is only One God. If they do not stop saying this, those who disbelieve among them will be afflicted with a painful punishment”*).

All are disobedience, 'iṣyān, and transgression, i'tidā'. The curse by David and Jesus therefore is for cumulative disobedience and transgression, not one act. The following verse will detail not forbidding evil as example.

---
## Verse 5:79
> They did not forbid one another from doing evil. Evil indeed was what they did

**A Community That Stopped Correcting Itself**

The verse states the failure in one sentence: they did not forbid one another from the wrong they were doing. That single habit is named here as the reason the curse of David and Jesus fell on them (5:78 — *“The disbelievers among the Children of Israel were condemned in the revelations of David and Jesus, son of Mary. That was for their disobedience and violations”*), and the sentence that follows it is the Book's verdict — evil indeed was what they did. The sūrah had already put the same charge to their scholars and rabbis, asking why they do not forbid the sinful speech and the unlawful gain around them (5:63 — *“Why do their rabbis and scholars not forbid them from saying what is sinful and consuming what is unlawful? Evil indeed is their inaction”*), and it had described the community racing each other into sin (5:62 — *“You see many of them racing towards sin, transgression, and consumption of forbidden gain. Evil indeed are their actions”*). A people who have stopped correcting each other have lost the last wall, and the wrong they were already doing then spreads unchecked.

**The Harmful Silence**

The verse's judgment is that silence in the face of evil is not neutrality. It is what allows the racing of the previous verses to continue, and it is what produced the friendships with the enemies of the community that the next verse condemns (5:80 — *“You see many of them taking the disbelievers as allies. Truly wicked are their misdeeds, which have earned them Allah’s wrath. And they will be in everlasting torment”*), together with the hostility and hatred the sūrah says was stirred among them (5:64 — *“We have stirred among them hostility and hatred until the Day of Judgment.”*). What the Book describes is a whole society slipping, with each man telling himself that the matter belongs to someone else, until the practice becomes ordinary and the correction becomes impossible. The verb form used in the verse is reciprocal: they did not forbid one another, which puts the duty on every man in the street as well as on the leadership.

**The Duty the Book Gives the Believers**

The community that follows this message is given the opposite instruction in the same Book: let there be among you a group calling to good, commanding what is right and forbidding what is wrong (3:104 — *“Let there be a group among you who call ˹others˺ to goodness, encourage what is good, and forbid what is evil—it is they who will be successful”*), and you are the best community brought out for people, commanding the right and forbidding the wrong (3:110 — *“Had the People of the Book believed, it would have been better for them.”*). The Prophet stated the duty in the plainest form: whoever sees a wrong should change it with his hand, and if he cannot, with his tongue, and if he cannot, with his heart, which is the weakest degree of faith (Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim 49). The sūrah supports the same duty from the other side when it tells the believers to help one another in righteousness and not in sin and aggression (5:2 — *“Cooperate with one another in goodness and righteousness, and do not cooperate in sin and transgression.”*), and when it makes them witnesses who stand for justice (5:8 — *“Do not let the hatred of a people lead you to injustice.”*). What this verse adds is the warning that the duty is not optional: it is what keeps a community's religion from becoming history.

---

## Verse 5:80
> You see many of them taking the disbelievers as allies. Truly wicked are their misdeeds, which have earned them Allah’s wrath. And they will be in everlasting torment

**Taking Disbelievers as Allies as Observed Evil**

Tarā kathīran minhum yatawallawna alladhīna kafarū reports taking disbelievers as allies. Tarā, you see, kathīran minhum, many of them, yatawallawna, allying, taking as guardians, alladhīna kafarū, those who disbelieve, indicates many among Children of Israel and hypocrites take disbelievers, polytheists, as allies, awliyā', guardians, protectors, as verse 51 prohibited taking Jews and Christians as exclusive guardians and verse 57 prohibited taking mockers as guardians. The same observation appears in *“You see many taking disbelievers as allies”* (5:80) itself and in *“You see those with sickness racing for guardianship”* (5:52) for similar alliance out of fear. The taking as allies is for protection, as they fear turn of fortune, dā'irah (5:52 — *“You see those with sickness in their hearts racing for their guardianship, saying ˹in justification˺, “We fear a turn of fortune will strike us.””*), and for worldly gain, as they consume forbidden gain (5:62 — *“You see many of them racing towards sin, transgression, and consumption of forbidden gain. Evil indeed are their actions”*). The verse therefore shows that taking disbelievers as allies is observable evil, tarā, you see, that should have been forbidden but was not, as previous verse said they did not forbid evil.

**Wicked Are Misdeeds, Earned Wrath**

La-bi'sa mā qaddamat lahum anfusuhum a sakhiṭa Allāhu 'alayhim — wicked are misdeeds that earned Allah's wrath — condemns. La-bi'sa, evil indeed, wicked, mā qaddamat lahum anfusuhum, what their selves forwarded for them, qaddamat from q-d-m, to forward, send ahead, anfusuhum, selves, indicates that their alliance with disbelievers is what they forwarded for themselves as deeds that will meet them in Hereafter. An sakhiṭa Allāhu 'alayhim, that Allah's wrath upon them, sakhiṭa from s-kh-ṭ, to be angry, wrath, indicates that misdeeds earned Allah's wrath, as *“Earned Allah's wrath”* (5:80) itself. The same phrasing appears for those who earned wrath, as in *“They earned wrath upon wrath”* (2:90) for similar. The wrath is for taking disbelievers as allies instead of believers, which is ẓulm and fisq, as earlier verses said. The misdeeds are described as wicked, la-bi'sa, showing strong condemnation.

**A Verse That Links Alliance to Wrath and Everlasting Punishment**

This verse links taking disbelievers as allies to earning Allah's wrath and everlasting torment, showing severity. The link appears also for those who take Jews and Christians as exclusive guardians in verse 51: whoever does so is among them, Allah does not guide wrongdoing people, and for those who race for guardianship out of fear in verses 52-53: deeds void, become losers. The verse therefore continues theme of loyalty: true guardians are Allah, Messenger, believers (5:55 — *“Your only guardians are Allah, His Messenger, and fellow believers—who establish prayer and pay alms-tax with humility”*), Allah's party prevails (5:56 — *“Whoever allies themselves with Allah, His Messenger, and fellow believers, then it is certainly Allah’s party that will prevail”*), do not take mockers as guardians (5:57 — *“And be mindful of Allah if you are ˹truly˺ believers”*), and taking disbelievers as allies earns wrath and everlasting torment.

Yatawallawna alladhīna kafarū, taking disbelievers as allies, is betrayal of believers, because guardianship should be for Allah, Messenger, believers (5:55 — *“Your only guardians are Allah, His Messenger, and fellow believers—who establish prayer and pay alms-tax with humility”*), and Allah's party prevails (5:56 — *“Whoever allies themselves with Allah, His Messenger, and fellow believers, then it is certainly Allah’s party that will prevail”*). The misdeeds forwarded, mā qaddamat lahum anfusuhum, are what they send ahead to Hereafter, and if wicked, lead to wrath, sakhiṭa Allāhu 'alayhim, and everlasting torment, khālidūn fī al-'adhāb, as verse says.

---
## Verse 5:81

> Had they believed in Allah, the Prophet, and what has been revealed to him, they would have never taken those ˹pagans˺ as allies. But most of them are rebellious

**Belief Would Have Prevented Alliance With Disbelievers**

Wa law kānū yu'minūna bi-Llāhi wa al-nabiyyi wa mā unzila ilayhi mā ittakhadhūhum awliyā' states conditional: had they believed, they would not have taken disbelievers as allies. The conditional law, had they believed, shows that true belief in Allah, Prophet, and revelation prevents taking disbelievers as allies, because belief requires loyalty to Allah, Messenger, believers, as verse 55 defined true guardians. The same conditional appears in *“Had they believed in Allah, Prophet, and what was revealed, they would not have taken them as allies”* (5:81) itself. The verse therefore explains why many took disbelievers as allies: lack of belief.

**Most Are Rebellious as Reason**

Wa lākinna kathīran minhum fāsiqūn — but many of them are rebellious — gives reason: many are fāsiqūn, rebellious, exiting obedience, as earlier verses said many are rebellious (5:49, 59, 81 — *“If they turn away ˹from Allah’s judgment˺, then know that it is Allah’s Will to repay them for some of their sins, and that many people are indeed rebellious”*). Kathīran minhum, many of them, fāsiqūn, rebellious, indicates that majority among those who took disbelievers as allies are rebellious, not true believers, so they take allies out of rebellion, not faith. The same phrase appears in *“Many of them are rebellious”* (5:81) itself and in similar verses. The rebellion includes breaking covenant, distorting, forgetting portion, racing to sin, not forbidding evil, saying Allah tight-fisted, etc., all forms of fisq. The verse therefore links alliance with disbelievers to fisq: those who are rebellious take disbelievers as allies, while those who believe take Allah, Messenger, believers as guardians and prevail.

**Why Belief in Prophet and Revelation Prevents False Alliance**

Belief in Allah, Prophet, and what was revealed prevents false alliance because revelation commands taking true guardians and not taking mockers and disbelievers as exclusive guardians, as verses 51, 55, 57 commanded. Because revelation promises protection from Allah, as verse 11 said let believers put trust in Allah and verse 67 said Allah will protect you from people, so seeking protection from disbelievers is unnecessary for believers. The alliance with disbelievers is for fear of turn of fortune, dā'irah (5:52 — *“You see those with sickness in their hearts racing for their guardianship, saying ˹in justification˺, “We fear a turn of fortune will strike us.””*), and for worldly gain, suḥt (5:62 — *“You see many of them racing towards sin, transgression, and consumption of forbidden gain. Evil indeed are their actions”*), which believers who trust Allah do not need. The verse therefore explains that true believers do not need false alliances because they trust Allah's protection and seek wasīlah, means to Allah, through good deeds, as verse 35 said. The next verse will describe most bitter toward believers are Jews and polytheists and most gracious are Christians, because priests and monks among them and not arrogant, showing that among disbelievers, some are more hostile, some more gracious.

**Faith as Preventer of False Alliance**

Yu'minūna bi-Llāhi wa al-nabiyyi wa mā unzila ilayhi, belief in Allah, Prophet.
